- [x] #832 {difficulty:4} [water 2/shading] Realistic water shading + gameplay query. On the #831 surface: PBR microfacet + Schlick FRESNEL between reflection and refraction; reflection = `sky.radiance(reflect)` always + SSR (screen-space reflection from the G-buffer depth/normal) on the desktop tier — NO planar reflection (a second scene render is the one thing tiled/mobile GPUs cannot afford; SSR + sky covers it, Boat Attack made the same call); refraction = lit-scene-colour read (the #842-#845 hdrColor copy) offset by the normal, MASKED by depth so objects above the surface do not bleed, with Beer-Lambert absorption `exp(-depth * absorbColour)`; TAA (opt-in) interaction checked. Gameplay: `waterHeightAt(x, y, t)` — CPU Gerstner mirror (exact for Gerstner, approximate for FFT) and/or a low-res displacement readback every N frames; BUOYANCY as an ECS query (`loop let entity, transform: mod Transform3D, buoyant: view Buoyant in query2(...)` sets Z), never a callback. Screenshot cases per tier. Depends on #831. Reference: docs/water-rendering-design.md §3.2 + §4 (Crest's query API shape; UE5 Water plugin data model — design only, EULA). STATUS (#848): #830 and #831 slice 1 have LANDED, so this is RUNNABLE NOW on the toon/Gerstner surface — Fresnel-blended sky reflection, refraction from the #842 litCopy offset by the normal and MASKED by depth, Beer-Lambert absorption, the TAA on/off check, waterHeightAt as a CPU Gerstner mirror, and buoyancy as the #807 query2 loop with an ECS test. Note from #830: the opaque snapshot is currently taken AFTER the water (fine while refraction is off) — split transparentPass so the snapshot precedes waterDraw (two lines) before reading litCopy. SSR and the FFT displacement readback need the realistic surface and are split out to #854. Slices, each a screenshot gate: (1) reflection + refraction + absorption; (2) TAA check; (3) waterHeightAt; (4) buoyancy query + test. DONE — all four slices on the toon/Gerstner surface (the SSR + FFT-readback items are #854 as planned). (1) Reflection + refraction + absorption: waterSurface.wgsl now weights the stylised-sky reflection by Schlick Fresnel (F0 = 0.02, replacing the ad-hoc pow^4); with WaterBody.refraction = 1 (new field, default 0 = mobile alpha path; refractStrength, absorbColor per-channel 1/m) the fragment reads the #842 opaque snapshot (litCopy bound as texture_2d + a linear clamped sampler, bindings 3/4, generation-keyed) at the screen uv offset by the normal, DEPTH-MASKED (the refracted pixel's depth is compared to the surface; if it is closer the pixel is an object above the water and the shader falls back to the unrefracted uv — the Boat Attack/Crest trick), then Beer-Lambert exp(-refrDepth * absorb) with the absorbed light replaced by scattered deep colour; a refracting body composes its own background so it is drawn OPAQUE (alpha 1), foam and the stepped highlight on top. Uniform grew to 16 vec4 (refract on/strength/lit size, absorb). The #830 ordering note is resolved: TransparentForward gained transparentSnapshot(cache) so an app takes the opaque copy BEFORE waterDraw (114 and 118 do; transparentPass still snapshots itself for apps without water, via a per-frame snapshotDone flag). (2) TAA check: 118 has RAE_WATER_TAA=1 (gbSetTaaEnabled); the TAA-on and FXAA frames are indistinguishable — no smear or ghost — because the surface applies the frame jitter LAST like the G-buffer; caveat: the deterministic frame holds the camera still, so this is the static case. (3) waterHeightAt(body, x, y, t) in Water.rae: the CPU mirror of the WGSL gerstner (same constants), honouring waveCount, with the horizontal Gerstner displacement inverted by two fixed-point iterations of p = (x,y) - dispXY(p); unit case 752_water_height pins flat = still level, crest/trough/period of a pure sine, travel with speed, and steep two-wave bounds. (4) Buoyancy as a QUERY: lib/water/Buoyancy.rae — Buoyant { offset } + buoyancySystem(transforms, buoyants, body, time) = the #807 query2 loop over Transform3d + Buoyant setting Z = waterHeightAt + offset, never a callback; unit case 753_water_buoyancy pins boat-on-crest+offset, untouched rock, buoy-in-trough, query == system. 118 enables refraction (RAE_WATER_REFRACTION=0 shows the mobile path); 114 stays on the alpha tier. Verified: 752/753 PASS; gates 114 + 118 PASS; zero validation errors in both the refraction and TAA runs; screenshot by eye: the bed 6 m down absorbed to blue-green, lighter shallows at the islands, island tops crisp with no bleed into the water. docs/water-rendering-design.md status updated. Not done here (by design): PBR microfacet (GGX) specular belongs to the realistic surface (#851), SSR and the FFT readback are #854.

Underwater: a post step (fog/absorption tint + optional caustics) when the camera is below a WaterBody surface. Fits the biome/terrain work (docs/biome-terrain-system.md). Depends on #830 (toon rivers work without #831). Reference: docs/water-rendering-design.md §5 Phase 3. STATUS (#848): #830 has LANDED (lib/water WaterBody/WaterSystem + the toon surface), so this is RUNNABLE NOW; toon rivers do not need the FFT. In place for it: terrainHeightAt (banks), the #843 transparent pass + #842 litCopy (underwater tint reads the lit frame), lib/Particles.rae for spray. Slices, each a screenshot gate: (1) RiverSpline component + one-time river MESH bake along the spline (Waterways technique, MIT — technique only), bank verts snapped to terrainHeightAt with a blend margin; (2) FLOW MAP + foam map baked from spline tangents/curvature; (3) the result as a WaterBody with flow-map-advected UVs (two-phase to hide the reset) and baked foam; (4) underwater: camera below the surface -> fullscreen Beer-Lambert tint/fog after lighting, optional sun caustics. DONE — slices 1-3 as one integrated river feature; slice 4 (underwater) filed as #855. lib/water/River.rae: RiverSpline (cubic bezier, bezierPoint/bezierTangent) and bakeRiverMesh(spline, width, samples, across, heights, bankMargin) -> MeshData, ONCE: `samples` rows along the flow x `across` quads across; uv = (arc length, offset across) in METRES so the ripple noise keeps the lake's scale; the normal attribute — which the water shader ignores because it builds its own — packs (flow tangent xy, curvature foam weight = tangent turn per metre x width, 0..1). Banks: the two edge rows take caller-supplied heights (3 per sample: left bank, surface, right bank) and a bank BELOW the surface tucks the edge to bank - margin so it hides under the ground; a bank above simply lets the flat surface run into it (depth test + shoreline foam). The heights are DATA the caller samples from its own heightfield — Rae has no callbacks and the bake needs none. Flow map + foam map (slice 2) are baked INTO THE MESH rather than textures: on a spline-parametrised ribbon the u coordinate IS the flow map, so the shader advects the ripple noise along u at flowSpeed with no texture and no phase reset to hide (the two-phase trick exists for texture flow maps on arbitrary meshes; not needed here). WaterBody gained meshId (0 = shared unit grid; >0 = its own world-space mesh) and flowSpeed; WaterSystem draws the body's mesh; the WGSL has a river mode (tuning.z): base = p, no Gerstner, noise coordinates (u - flowSpeed*t, v), baked foam from n.z streaked by the ripple noise and max-ed into the foam term. createRiverToon(mesh, flowSpeed) sets river defaults (waveCount 0, depthMax 0.6, foamDistance 0.25, absorb 0.95). 114: examples/114_walker_character/River.rae bakes a 48-sample, 4 m wide stream across the meadow near the spawn with banks/surface from terrainHeightAt (surface = ground + 0.35 so there is a channel), added via waterAddBody after createApp; the 114 gate now asserts "[water] toon lake: 2 bodies". Unit case 754_river_bake pins vertex/index counts, arc-length u, across v, bank tuck-under vs stay-at-surface, tangent packing, zero foam on a straight run, foam on a bend, unit tangents. Verified: 754 PASS; 114 gate PASS; emit/link clean; zero validation errors. VISUAL: the first tuned frame showed NO river — a 12 cm-deep toon river over white snow is all foam (the shoreline cutoff is ~0 at that depth): white on white. A temporary opaque-magenta diagnostic (reverted, 0 leftovers) proved the ribbon snakes exactly where intended across the meadow; the final tune (surface +0.35, saturated palette, foam kept to the banks) gives a light-cyan stream with foam flecks along that path — pale against snow by nature (Fresnel pulls it toward the bright sky at grazing angles); on darker terrain it reads stronger. Two process trips recorded: an edit script died on a stale anchor after writing River.rae, and an EMPTY TEST= name launched the whole suite — the single-case invocation is now guarded on a non-empty name. docs/water-rendering-design.md status updated.
- [x] #849 {difficulty:4} [water 2/fft slice 2] Spectrum + butterfly for the FFT ocean (#831 decomposition; slice 1 — the waterFft graph node, cascade resources/textures and WaterBody tier fields — is DONE). In lib/water/waterFft.wgsl (composed like GrassCompute): a compute pass that fills a per-cascade h0 spectrum texture from a TMA/JONSWAP-style spectrum with directional (Hasselmann) spreading (wind speed/direction, fetch, cascade tile size -> k grid), run ONCE per resolution/params change, plus the Stockham butterfly texture (twiddles + read indices per stage, no bit-reversal) once per FFT size. Verify by a low-res READBACK (the grass diagnostics path): h0 is non-zero, Hermitian-symmetric, and its energy peaks at the JONSWAP peak frequency. Technique only from GodotOceanWaves / inkwell-webgpu-water — do NOT copy code (no license). DONE. lib/water/waterFft.wgsl (from the Tessendorf / Hasselmann / Bouws papers — no code copied): `spectrum` fills a cascade's h0 rgba32float storage texture: k = 2pi/L (x - N/2, y - N/2); JONSWAP S(w) (fetch-limited: w_p = 22 (g^2/(U F))^(1/3), alpha = 0.076 (U^2/(F g))^0.22, gamma 3.3, sigma 0.07/0.09) x the TMA finite-depth factor x Hasselmann 1980 spreading D = N(s) cos^(2s)(theta/2) with s(w/w_p) and the normalisation 2^(2s-1)/pi * G(s+1)^2 / G(2s+1) via a Stirling ln-Gamma; times the Jacobian (dw/dk)/k with w^2 = g k tanh(kh); amplitude sqrt(2E) dk; complex Gaussian from a PCG hash + Box-Muller seeded per (cascade, texel) so a texel is deterministic; k = 0 and the Nyquist row/column zeroed (their -k is themselves); packed (h0(k), h0(-k)) per texel. `butterfly` fills the per-output radix-2 Stockham table (log2N columns x N rows): stage s, span Ns: block = i/2Ns, k = i mod 2Ns, reads j = block*Ns + (k mod Ns) and j + N/2, twiddle exp(+2pi i (k mod Ns)/2Ns) with the sign folded for k >= Ns — inverse, autosort, no bit reversal. WaterSystem: ensureFftGpu builds two compute pipelines from the one module (each entry point gets its OWN pipeline + bind group — wgpu derives a layout from the bindings an entry point uses), a 3-vec4 FftU uniform, the butterfly texture, per-cascade h0 textures, a MapRead staging buffer; bakeFftSpectrum runs ONCE per params (waterFftInvalidate re-bakes), one uniform upload + one submitted dispatch per cascade, then the butterfly. Cascade bands: kLow/kHigh per cascade so the summed cascades never count a wavelength twice; hand-over at kHigh = cascadeBandBins(6) * 2pi/L_next — the readback showed the naive 2pi/L_next boundary gave the 250 m cascade a 4-cell (56-texel) disc of its 256x256 grid. WaterBody carries the sea state: windSpeed 9, windDirRad 0.7, fetch 250 km, waterDepth 50 (=> k_p 0.0603 rad/m, a 104 m peak wavelength). VERIFICATION by READBACK (the grass map-read idiom; blocking, diagnostics only): readbackFloat4 copies an rgba32float texture to the staging buffer with bytesPerRow padded to the 256-byte COPY_BYTES_PER_ROW_ALIGNMENT (the 8-wide butterfly needs it — the first run panicked exactly there) and maps it into a List(Float); waterFftDiagnose checks (a) non-zero texels match the band's k-space disc pi (kHigh/dk)^2 within 0.6..1.3x, (b) EXACT (k,-k) packing symmetry — texel(k).zw == texel(-k).xy, measured 0, (c) the radial energy peak bin vs the JONSWAP k_p bin — measured peak bin 2 vs expected 2.40 (within 2), (d) butterfly stage 0 reads (i/2, i/2 + N/2) with twiddle +-1 — true. 118 runs the diagnose under RAE_WATER_FFT_DIAG=1 at its second water frame and its gate now asserts "[water fft] h0 ok"; PASS. C: the water handle store widened to 64 slots (32.. = FFT state; C-surface gate ok). Verified: make build clean; gates 114 (bake runs there too) + 118 PASS. docs/water-rendering-design.md updated. Next: #850 evolution + Stockham iFFT (verify by iFFT of a KNOWN spectrum), then #851/#852/#854.
- [x] #850 {difficulty:4} [water 2/fft slice 3] Per-frame evolution + Stockham inverse FFT (#831 decomposition; needs #849). Compute passes: evolve h0 -> h(k,t) (dispersion w = sqrt(g k), plus the x/y choppiness and slope terms packed as extra complex channels), then the Stockham iFFT: row pass over the stages reading the butterfly texture, transpose, reuse the same row pass for columns; write displacement (x,y,z), normal and the Jacobian/foam map into the #831 cascade textures (rgba16float storage). VERIFY THE FFT HONESTLY: seed a KNOWN single-frequency spectrum, run the iFFT, read back one row and assert the expected sine (amplitude + period) — the only real correctness check; then the JONSWAP field for non-NaN, bounded displacement. Needs on-hardware iteration. DONE. lib/water/waterFft.wgsl gained the per-frame path: `evolve` — h(k,t) = h0(k) e^(iwt) + conj(h0(-k)) e^(-iwt) with w^2 = g k tanh(kh), then the field pair this dispatch packs as Z = A + iB: (h, Dx), (Dy, dh/dx), (dh/dy, dDx/dx), (dDy/dy, dDx/dy) with Dx = -i (kx/k) h etc. — TWO real fields per complex transform, valid because every field's spectrum is Hermitian (h(-k) = conj h(k)), so the inverse transform's real part is A and its imaginary part B; `stockham` — one radix-2 stage along x for every row, reading (twiddle, a, b) from the #849 butterfly column `stage`; `transpose`; the same stage shader for the columns; `fieldOut` un-transposes into the per-field result; `post` — undo the centred spectrum's (-1)^(x+y), chop by WaterBody.choppiness (new field, 1.2), normal from the slopes, Jacobian J = (1 + l dDx/dx)(1 + l dDy/dy) - l^2 dDx/dy^2, foam = clamp(1 - J) — into the #831 cascade maps (rgba16float storage). WaterFft.waterFftUpdate encodes ALL of it as ONE compute pass in ONE submit (dispatches in a pass see earlier storage writes): per cascade x field: evolve -> P, log2N stages ping-ponging P/Q, transpose, log2N stages, fieldOut -> R_f; then post; ~230 dispatches/frame for 3 x 256^2. Sources are bound as texture_2d<f32> (core textureLoad), only destinations as storage textures (no non-core storage reads); per-(cascade, field) evolve uniforms and per-stage uniforms because a single submit forbids rewriting one buffer between dispatches. Runs every frame only for body.style = realistic (toon bodies never read the maps). ARCHITECTURE: WaterSystem.rae crossed the 1000-line hard cap (the compiler enforces it) — the whole FFT (cascades, bake, frame, diagnostics, releases, waterShutdown) moved to lib/water/WaterFft.rae (765 lines; WaterSystem.rae 331); callers open water/WaterFft. C handle store widened to 160 slots. THE HONEST FFT TEST (waterFftDiagnoseIfft, run by 118 under RAE_WATER_FFT_DIAG=1 and asserted by its gate as "[water fft] ifft ok"): `spectrum` has a test mode that seeds ONE known wave (h0 = 0.25 at k index +-(3, 0), so h = 0.5 at both at t = 0) whose unnormalised inverse transform must be exactly cos(2pi 3x/N) of amplitude 1 — read back row N/2 of the height field, sign-corrected by (-1)^(x+y): MEASURED amplitude 1 (expect 1), value at x = 0 = 1 (expect 1), zero crossings 6 (expect 6), finite; then JONSWAP re-baked and transformed at t = 1: finite, height max 0.084 m (a 9 m/s sea on the 250 m tile). Both gates 114 + 118 PASS; zero validation errors. ON-HARDWARE FINDINGS (this slice needed the iteration the task predicted): (1) the first transform came back NaN at every texel; a probe that parked the uniform in a zeroed texel proved the uniform correct, and the NaN pattern (finite only in a 35-cell disc = k·h < 44) identified the GPU's exp-based tanh overflowing f32 past ~44 (k·depth reaches 160 on the 8 m tile at 50 m) — clamped to 20 in both evolve and the spectrum, where it was silently poisoning the 8 m cascade the h0 diagnostic never inspected; (2) a Rae function returning a c_struct by value emits an undefined `rae_WGPUBindGroupEntry` C type — filed as #856; the two helpers were inlined; (3) two syntax slips of my own (a `loop` step form, a local named `view`). docs/water-rendering-design.md updated. Next: #851 the realistic surface sampling + summing the cascades on a clip-map with whitecaps, then #852, #854.
- [x] #851 {difficulty:4} [water 2/fft slice 4] The realistic surface (#831 decomposition; needs #850). Extend lib/water/waterSurface.wgsl + WaterSystem: when body.style is realistic, sample and SUM the 3 cascades (tiles ~250/60/8 m) for displacement in the vertex shader and normal + Jacobian in the fragment; whitecaps where Jacobian < 0 (replace the toon foam term on this path; keep the shoreline depth foam); a camera-centred clip-map / projected-grid mesh so vertex density follows the camera (Crest's LOD idea) instead of the fixed 96x96 unit grid. Screenshot gate (an isolated example like 117, camera low over the ocean) + visual check. DONE. SURFACE: waterSurface.wgsl gained the realistic path (WaterStyle.realistic, uniform cascades.w): the vertex shader sums the three cascades' displacement sampled at world xy / tile (textureSampleLevel; cascade count from the uniform), skipping Gerstner; the fragment samples the cascades' normal maps and adds their SLOPES (-n.xy/n.z) into one normal, and turns the Jacobian channel into whitecaps with foam = clamp((0.5 - J) * 2): J = 1 is flat, J -> 0 is the surface folding, so the foam sits on the crests (the first pass flagged J < 1 and foamed everywhere — tuned by eye); the shoreline depth foam stays; shading is a smooth diffuse and a sharp smooth Blinn-Phong glint instead of the toon steps; refraction/Fresnel/absorption unchanged. The six cascade maps (displacement+J, normal per cascade) are bound at 5..10 on the surface bind group — the toon path binds them too (one layout) but never samples — and the bind is deferred until waterFftStep has allocated them; the cascade slot constants moved to WaterSystem.rae so it can bind them without a circular open. HARDWARE FINDING: the first ocean frame was hard-edged blocks and long streak bands — the cascades were sampled through the lit sampler (ClampToEdge, right for refraction) while world.xy / tile runs far outside [0,1]; a REPEAT sampler (binding 11, slot 4) fixed it in one step. MESH: lib/water/ClipMap.rae createRadialGridMesh(rings, segments, firstCell, growth) — concentric rings whose spacing grows geometrically (64 x 96, 1 m, 1.09 reaches ~2.5 km with ~6k vertices), Crest's LOD idea as ONE draw: WaterBody.meshMode 2 makes the vertex shader add the camera position snapped to whole metres (uniform centre.xy) to every vertex each frame, and because the cascades are looked up in WORLD space the waves stay put while the grid rides under the camera. meshMode replaces the meshId>0 river test (0 unit grid, 1 baked world mesh, 2 camera-centred); createOceanRealistic(surfaceZ) builds the body (ocean kind, realistic, refraction on, deep-sea palette, depthMax 12, glint power 180). EXAMPLE: 119_ocean_fft (+ raepack, "3D Renderer") — the ocean over a 40 m-deep bed, the shared CameraRig in free mode, comma/period time of day, deterministic test frame freezes the sea at t = 2 s; gate in run_examples asserts the "[ocean] deterministic deferred frame rendered: ... 3 cascades of 256, N ocean frames" line + "[water fft] spectrum baked" + non-blank. Verified: gates 114 + 118 + 119 PASS (114/118 exercise the toon path with the new bindings), zero validation errors; by eye (/tmp/ocean851c.png): long swells from the 250 m cascade, chop from the finer ones, whitecaps streaked along the crests, deep-blue troughs, Fresnel sky at grazing angles, a clean far horizon, no seams. docs/water-rendering-design.md updated. Next: #852 update-rate balancing + tiers + spray, #854 SSR + FFT readback.

- [x] #884 {difficulty:4} [compiler/ownership] A produced value passed straight to a `view`/`mod` parameter is never released — legal code that silently leaks. VERIFIED example (compiles and runs): given `func create(id: view Int) ret Slot`, `func drop(this: mod Slot)`, and `func inspect(slot: view Slot) ret Int { ret slot.id }`, the line `let seen: Int = inspect(slot: create(id: 1))` logs `acquire 1` then `seen 1` but NEVER `release 1`, while `let kept: Slot = create(id: 2)` followed by `inspect(slot: kept)` DOES log `release 2` at scope exit. The `create(id: 1)` result is an unnamed temporary that no binding owns, so no drop is emitted for it; the same holds for any heap-owning temporary (a `String`/`List`-returning call passed to `view`) today, not just destructor types. FIX: drop such temporaries at the end of the statement — the string-pool mark/flush already brackets expression statements (`{ int __rae_spm = rae_string_pool_mark(); ...; rae_string_pool_flush(__rae_spm); }`); bracket struct temporaries that have a destructor or own heap the same way, dropped after the statement's value is consumed. Test with a fake-resource counter asserting acquire count == release count. — DONE: every statement is emitted through a buffer; a produced value borrowed by a `view` parameter that owns heap or has a destructor becomes a named statement temporary (declared before the statement, dropped after it; a `ret` drops its temporaries before the locals). Temporaries follow the local rule: struct literal = full drop, call result = alias drop (destructors and containers run, String fields skipped — the first cut double-freed JsonValue aliases in the glTF loaders). Test 768 (fake-resource counter: expr stmt, let, assign, if condition, ret, generic callee, produced list); 766 now shows the release. Suite 428/0, examples 70/0. Loop-condition temporaries and break/continue out of an if are #886.
- [x] #885 {difficulty:4} [compiler/ownership] `create` gaps after #880: (a) a `create(...)` in a literal field of a GENERIC struct whose field type is still `T` is not resolved (the field type comes from the template, not the instance); (b) `create` as an operand with no expected type (`log(create(...))`, binary operators) is only caught at statement level and in `Any` parameters; (c) sema-level use-after-drop is a lexical scan of one statement list, not dataflow across branches. Decide and implement. — DONE: (a) generic-instance literal fields substitute the instance's type arguments before choosing the constructor, nested bare literals included; (b) a post-pass over every function body reports any `create` no position bound, without double-reporting ones the resolver already rejected — it also exposed that arguments of generic container methods (`list.add(value: create(...))`) and of generic calls with a positional type argument (`pick(Slot, value: create(...))`) were never typed by sema at all, now bound from the callee's type arguments; (c) use-after-drop is a dataflow over the whole body (branches merge by union, loop bodies walked twice, let/assign revive, drop-of-dropped is a use) paired with a runtime live flag per explicitly dropped local so a drop in one branch releases exactly once on every path. Tests 769/770/771 new, 767 extended, 763 wording; suite 431/0, examples 70/0.
- [x] #886 {difficulty:4} [compiler/ownership] Temporaries made in a `loop` CONDITION are released once, after the loop, not per iteration (#884 follow-up; verified: `loop inspect(slot: create(id: i)) < 2 { i = i + 1 }` logs acquire 0/1/2 but only release 2). Also a `break`/`continue` out of an `if` whose condition produced a temporary skips that temporary's drop. Fix: drop the condition's statement temporaries at the top of each iteration body / before the re-test, and on the non-local exit paths (the loop_body_local_start machinery already runs local drops there). Test with the fake-resource counter from 768. — DONE: every statement temporary carries a `_set` flag raised by the producing expression and consumed by its guarded drop, so a drop can be written at several points safely; the loop emitter drops the loop's own condition temporaries at the end of each iteration, break/continue drop every enclosing statement's temporaries up to and including the loop's (new parent link between statement temporaries), and a short-circuited condition that never produced its value never drops one. Test 772 (loop condition, break and continue through an if condition, short-circuit and/or, nested loops: every acquire has one release); suite 432/0, examples 70/0. No known limits remain open against the lifecycle design.

Inference-free, so it works in `ret`, inside generic functions and as an argument. Steps: (1) compiler — extend the `Type.create(...)` resolution (#880) to accept an unparameterised qualifier and take the type param from the first positional argument, disambiguating List/StringMap/IntMap by the qualifier (they share the `(<type>, cap)` shape); (2) rename the four constructors to `create`, delete the #880 twins; (3) switch the three Compiled collection-literal hooks (c_discovery.c ~219, c_stmt.c ~1739, c_expr.c ~1254) from name=="createList" to name=="create" disambiguated by a `List` return type (deprecated VM lookups stay); (4) sweep the ~1000 call sites `createX(T, args)` -> `X.create(T, args)`; (5) update tests 647 and 562. Full suite and example gate green per commit. Does not depend on #889. — DONE: `List.create(EntityId, cap: N)` (and StringMap/IntMap/ComponentTable) implemented as a pre-sema AST desugar to the internal per-type constructor `createList(...)`, run over EVERY function body incl. generic templates (which sema does not analyse and clones verbatim per specialisation), so the backend's positional-type-arg specialisation binds each call's own element type correctly inside generic functions (the #889 failure mode of inferred `create`). ~1000 call sites swept to `X.create(Type, args)`; 60 no-type `createList(cap:N)` sites gained their element type from the binding; tests 647/562 regenerated. DEVIATION: the four constructor functions are kept as INTERNAL desugar targets, not deleted — a shared `create` cannot be disambiguated by qualifier after a generic body is cloned (clone_expr drops decl_link), so deletion is impossible while keeping generic-body correctness; the inference-free `List.create` surface syntax (the decided goal) is fully delivered. DONE. Closed the two #896-identified pointer-visibility gaps in sema.c, without over-restricting legal whole-owner moves. (a) A whole-value COPY of a raw-Ptr aggregate with no lifecycle now requires unsafe: (i) `let ys: List(Ptr) = xs` / a plain `struct { p: Ptr }` copied from a place — added to the let-stmt handler beside the destructor-copy check, gated on sema_expr_copies_place + sema_type_contains_ptr + NOT a bare Ptr value (already caught by the read guard) + NOT uncopyable-with-destructor (that louder error already fires) + no user `copy` fn; (ii) `Array(Ptr, cap: N)` value construction — a new expr-level branch beside the object-literal guard, matched by callee ident "Array" so user factory functions returning a Ptr aggregate stay safe. (b) IMPLICIT boxing into Any at a ret/assignment/Any-arg now requires unsafe — the check is at the implicit-box insertion in ensure_type_match (the explicit-box operand was already guarded in sema_analyze_expr, but the implicit box is minted there and never re-analysed); one site covers ret, assignment and Any arguments. own transfers, view/mod borrows, fresh factory-call results, and types with a user `func copy` all stay safe (verified). - [x] #899 {difficulty:2} [collections/ownership bug] Fix a pre-existing latent double-free in `lib/Files.rae` `listDirByExt` (and any sibling using the same shape): it does `let f: File = rae_ext_rae_buf_get(buf: all.data, index: i)` — a SHALLOW copy aliasing the source list's String storage — then `result.add(value: f)`, so the returned `List(File)` shares String buffers with the local `all` list; dropping both (any caller that keeps the result and lets it fall out of scope) frees the same pointers twice and aborts (`___BUG_IN_CLIENT_OF_LIBMALLOC_POINTER_BEING_FREED_WAS_NOT_ALLOCATED` in `rae_core_List_drop_rae_File`). Reproduced against the original code (predates #894; NOT caused by the unsafe migration, which only wrapped the existing statements). No current test/example triggers it because callers consume the result without dropping it. Fix by deep-copying the element into the result (e.g. build the `File` with copied `name`/`folder`, or add via a proper owned accessor) rather than adding the alias; add a regression that builds the filtered list and drops it. Discovered during #894. NO VISUAL TESTS: do not run run_examples.sh, make test-examples, any screenshot gate or any example in a window — the default make test / watch-tests.sh is the non-visual unit suite and that is the only test run allowed; if a visual check is needed, end with a note asking the user to run `make test-examples` by hand. DONE. Reproduced the exact double-free against the pre-fix code (SIGABRT, `___BUG_IN_CLIENT_OF_LIBMALLOC_POINTER_BEING_FREED_WAS_NOT_ALLOCATED` in `rae_core_List_drop_rae_File_...`, matching the task's cited crash). Root cause: the compiler's is_owned-clearing for an alias local only fires when the LET's declared type is exactly `String` (c_stmt.c); `File` (a struct with String fields) doesn't get that treatment, so `f`'s nested `name`/`folder` heap pointers kept is_owned=1 after the raw `buf_get`, and `result.add(value: f)` copied that alias byte-for-byte into `result` — both `all` and `result` then owned the same String heap. Fixed by adding a deep-copied struct literal (`{ name: f.name, folder: f.folder }`) instead of the alias, the same mechanism `listDir` already uses to build its own entries. Verified via git-stash bisection: crashes before, passes after. Found and fixed one sibling of the same alias-into-another-owning-list shape: `lib/ui/ThemeResolved.rae`'s `applyResolvedTokens` read `src.spaceNames`/`radiusNames`/`padNames` via raw `buf_get` then added the alias to `dst` (its own container loop, just below, already used the safe `if let ... copyAt` idiom) — switched the three broken loops to the same safe idiom and dropped the now-unnecessary outer `unsafe {}` block; verified compiling + running correctly by probe (String-typed lets get a narrower is_owned-clearing treatment than File did, so this one didn't reproduce a same-scope crash, but the raw-alias version left `dst` silently sharing `src`'s heap — a use-after-free hazard for the real call sites, where `dst` (a UiWorld's persistent theme) outlives `src`; the fix gives `dst` genuinely independent storage, matching the function's own documented contract). Audited every other `rae_ext_rae_buf_get` site in lib/ for the same shape (Sys.rae/Layer.rae/Debug.rae/LayoutSystem.rae add `Int`/`EntityId`, plain values, safe; Json.rae's bulk-transfer already zeroes the source list's length before drop, a different, already-safe pattern; StringMap/IntMap resize already deep-copies via `rae_ext_rae_string_copy` before storing) — no further instances found. New regression `compiler/tests/cases/806_files_list_dir_by_ext_no_double_free`: builds a deterministic fixtures/ subdirectory (2 `.txt` + 1 `.dat`), calls `listDir` then `listDirByExt`, drops both at scope exit (the exact double-free shape), and self-checks the filtered count against a manual scan of `all` (so it doesn't depend on a hard-coded directory listing, which stray files like `.DS_Store` could make flaky) — confirmed it fails (crashes) against the pre-fix code and passes after. Full non-visual unit suite via watch-tests.sh: 466 passed, 0 failed, exit 0 (includes the new 806 case and both fixed files).

- [x] #901 {difficulty:2} [compiler/backend bug] `List(Ptr).copyAt(index:)` miscompiles: the generic List accessor emits the struct-rep optional form (`__opt.has = 1; __opt.value = ...`) for its `opt Ptr` result, but `opt Ptr` lowers to a bare `Buffer_void` (`void*`), so gcc rejects the generated C ("member reference base type 'Buffer_void' is not a structure or union"). Reproduce: a struct with a `handles: List(Ptr)` field, `handles.add(value: p)` inside unsafe, then `if let raw: Ptr = handles.copyAt(index: 0)` — fails at C compile. Pre-existing; found while implementing #869, which works around it by reading/writing handles through the raw `rae_ext_rae_buf_get`/`rae_ext_rae_buf_set(V: Ptr, ...)` accessors inside unsafe blocks. Fix: give the Ptr-element accessors (copyAt/get/first/last…) the correct `opt Ptr` representation consistently with the rest of the opt lowering (or make opt Ptr struct-rep), then switch gpu/GpuResources back to `copyAt`; add a regression case. Related to the #898 Ptr-aggregate copy gap, but this one reaches INVALID C. Deps: none. NO VISUAL TESTS: do not run run_examples.sh, make test-examples, any screenshot gate or any example in a window — the default make test / watch-tests.sh is the non-visual unit suite and that is the only test run allowed; if a visual check is needed, end with a note asking the user to run `make test-examples` by hand. DONE. Root cause: TWO compounding bugs. (1) In BOTH mangler.c functions (mangle_type_recursive and mangle_type_recursive_specialized), the `Ptr -> "Buffer_void"` name shortcut ran BEFORE the `is_opt` check — `is_opt` is a flag on the SAME AstTypeRef, so `get_base_type_name(opt Ptr)` still returns "Ptr", and the shortcut fired first, naming `opt Ptr` the IDENTICAL "Buffer_void" name plain `Ptr` gets (a bare `void*` typedef, not a struct) instead of "rae_opt_Buffer_void". Fixed by checking `is_opt` first in both functions (it recurses into the payload, which then correctly hits the Ptr shortcut alone). (2) The struct-rep DECISION (rae_typeinfo_opt_is_struct_rep in c_backend.c) excluded TYPE_BUFFER (RaeAny), but the name-based fallback used when a generic specialization's concrete-arg typeref carries no resolved TypeInfo (mangler_opt_is_struct_rep/mangler_payload_is_struct_rep in mangler.c, and type_mangle_recursive's TYPE_OPT case in type.c) did NOT exclude "Ptr" — so copyAt's actual codegen path fell to struct-rep=true via the fallback while the TypeInfo path would have said RaeAny: a three-way inconsistency the file's own #238 comment warns about. Resolved by adding TYPE_BUFFER to all three switch statements so `opt Ptr` (and any `opt Buffer(T)`, currently unused elsewhere) is consistently struct-rep everywhere. Verified: reproduced the exact task repro (struct with `handles: List(Ptr)`, add inside unsafe, `if let raw: Ptr = handles.copyAt(index: 0)`) and the exact gcc error before the fix; after the fix it compiles and a full round-trip check (two distinct malloc'd addresses stored, read back at the right index via `is`/`is not` identity comparison, out-of-range index gives `none`) passes. New regression compiler/tests/cases/807_list_ptr_copyat_opt_rep mirrors the task's exact repro shape; confirmed it fails with the identical gcc error against the pre-fix compiler (git-stash bisection) and passes after. Switched gpu/GpuResources.slotHandle's internal read from the raw rae_ext_rae_buf_get(V: Ptr, ...) workaround to the now-fixed copyAt (writes still use rae_ext_rae_buf_set, which List has no checked equivalent for); confirmed no other opt Ptr / opt Buffer(T) usage exists anywhere else in lib/ to audit. Updated the outdated workaround note in docs/gpu-resource-identity-design.md. NO VISUAL TESTS run. Full non-visual watch-tests unit suite: 467 passed, 0 failed, exit 0 (466 prior + the new 807 case).
- [x] #902 {difficulty:2} [compiler/ownership codegen bug] An explicit consuming `x.drop()` on a `var x: T` local (T with a user destructor) inside one `if let` block makes codegen emit the #885 runtime live-flag guard `if (__rae_live_<name>) { ... }` for a DIFFERENT local of the same type in a SIBLING `if let` block that is never explicitly dropped — but never declares that flag, so gcc fails with "use of undeclared identifier '__rae_live_doomed'". Reproduce: two sibling `if let ... { var a: Recording = ...; a.drop() }` / `if let ... { var doomed: Recording = ...; (no drop) }` blocks in one function with `func drop(this: mod Recording)` — the second block's scope-exit drop is emitted as a flag-guarded drop without the flag declaration. Found while writing 795_gpu_lifetime_model (#870), which now abandons by scope exit instead. Fix: scope the explicit-drop live flag to the exact local (declare it where that local is declared) instead of keying on the type/name across sibling scopes; add a regression. Deps: none. NO VISUAL TESTS: do not run run_examples.sh, make test-examples, any screenshot gate or any example in a window — the default make test / watch-tests.sh is the non-visual unit suite and that is the only test run allowed; if a visual check is needed, end with a note asking the user to run `make test-examples` by hand. DONE. Root cause: the runtime live-flag `int __rae_live_<name>` (c_stmt.c, #885) is tracked per LOCAL SLOT INDEX in CFuncContext.local_drop_flag[], and a slot index is REUSED across sibling scopes within one function — an if-let/loop/match-case body's locals pop back to `saved_locals` on scope exit, so the next sibling scope's first local claims the SAME index. Three of the seven let-declaration sites in emit_stmt_inner already reset local_drop_flag[idx]=false when a new local claims a slot (if-let binding, opt-box payload unwrap, collection-loop element); the other four (the general let/var path, a materialised-bind ref, a mod-alias-to-array-element ref, and a collection-literal-initialized local) did not — so a local explicitly `.drop()`-ed in one sibling scope (flag declared + set true) left that stale `true` for whatever unrelated local next occupied the slot; the scope-exit drop pass then emitted `if (__rae_live_<newName>) {...}` guarding the NEW local's name, but that flag was never declared for it (gcc: use of undeclared identifier). Fixed by adding the same `local_drop_flag[idx] = false` reset at all four missing sites, matching the existing pattern — the flag is now always scoped to the exact local it is declared for, never leaked from whatever local last occupied the slot. Audited match-statement case bodies too (case pattern list.c: 2647): they save/restore local_count exactly like if-let and route through the SAME emit_stmt LET paths, so no separate fix was needed there — verified directly with a match-case sibling repro. Verified: reproduced the task's exact repro (sibling if-let with `var a: Recording ... a.drop()` then `var doomed: Recording` with no drop) and its exact gcc error before the fix; after, both drop exactly once. Also verified four alternating explicit/implicit sibling if-let blocks and a match-case sibling variant, all correct. New regression compiler/tests/cases/808_sibling_if_let_explicit_drop_flag (four alternating sibling if-lets); bisected against the pre-fix compiler via git stash — it reproduces the identical undeclared-identifier error (7 errors, flags leaking through TWO further sibling blocks) and passes after the fix. - [x] #903 {difficulty:5} [gpu/render] Prerequisite for #873 (maintainer chose option A): add render support to the #869/#870 manager so a render surface can be manager-owned. RenderPipelineId + createRenderPipeline (WGSL vertex/fragment entry points, a vertex buffer layout, colour target format + alpha blend, optional reverse-Z read-only depth) with ordinary create/retire; render bind groups built from wgpuRenderPipelineGetBindGroupLayout over an ORDERED entry list (buffers, texture views, samplers and adopted externals at consecutive bindings — the compute createBindGroup's fixed buffers-then-views-then-samplers order cannot express water's layout); render-pass recording over #870's Recording (colour attachment with load/clear/store, optional read-only depth attachment, set pipeline/bind group/vertex + index buffers, indexed draw) with the same use validation and pinning as compute; and an ADOPTED-EXTERNAL kind: ExternalId over a native handle the manager references (bind, attach, pin) but NEVER owns or releases — the way to reference the legacy gbuffer's per-frame resources (gbFrameUbuf/gbViewDepth/gbLitView/gbLitCopyView, mesh vbuf/ibuf) without migrating the whole renderer. No new C; generated bindings only; C-surface gate. Verify on hardware: draw a known-colour triangle into a manager RGBA8 texture, copy it to a buffer, read it back through a ReadbackId and check the pixels; bind an adopted external buffer; deterministic slot/pin rules stay covered by 795. Then #873 migrates water onto it. Deps: #872. — DONE: new lib/gpu/GpuRender.rae (render surface over the #869/#870 manager, pure Rae over the bindings, no new C): kinds kindRenderPipeline=8 (released via wgpuRenderPipelineRelease in drop order) and kindExternal=9 (adopted, NEVER released by the manager — releaseHandle has no branch, releaseExternal only bumps generation + frees the slot); RenderPipelineId + createRenderPipeline(wgsl, vertexEntry, fragmentEntry, RenderPipelineSpec{vertexStride, ordered VertexAttributeSpec list, colorFormat, alphaBlend (water's straight-alpha blend), depthFormat 0=none / depthWrite / depthCompare (water = Depth32Float, no write, Greater), cullMode}); ExternalId + adoptExternalBuffer/adoptExternalTextureView (unsafe: adopter keeps the handle valid, re-adopts on targets-generation change); ResourceRef (bufferRef/textureViewRef/samplerRef/externalRef) as the one way to name manager resources or externals for ordered bind entries, attachments, vertex/index buffers (external sub-kind checked against the binding use, sizes from native re-query); createRenderBindGroup(pipeline, entries) via wgpuRenderPipelineGetBindGroupLayout group 0, consecutive bindings in list order, yields an ordinary BindGroupId pinning every entry; RenderTarget (colour view clear/load + optional read-only/read-write depth view) and recordDrawIndexed over #870's Recording (begin pass, set pipeline/bind group/vertex/u32 index buffers, DrawIndexed, end; Vertex/Index usage and index-buffer size checked; every ref + bind-group deps become uses, pinned to completion); recordCopyTextureToBuffer (RGBA8 level 0 → buffer, 256-aligned pitch) as the pixel readback path. Pins for both new kinds wired in GpuLifetime; tables in GpuResources.create/drop/tableFor. Hardware check examples/zz_gpu_render_check: clip-space triangle → 4x4 RGBA8 manager texture, fragment colour from an ADOPTED uniform buffer the check owns (packed via GpuArgs), CopyTextureToBuffer → ReadbackId → all 16 pixels == (51,102,153,255); then retire pipeline, release external (adopter still owns + releases its buffer), and refusals (released external in a bind group → none; stale pipeline draw → false; 100-byte row pitch → false) — passes (exit 0). Docs: "Implementation notes (#903)" in docs/gpu-resource-identity-design.md. C-surface gate ok (16 symbols). Full watch-tests gate: green (534 pass, 0 fail, exit 0; unit + example gate incl. zz_gpu_render_check). Unblocks #873.

- [x] #911 {difficulty:5} figure out how we should bring ”rae format” into this repo. it should fit all into 100 chars per line, and 1000 lines per file ofc. - And function parameters to their own lines, was it after 4 parameters or so? Or at least before 100 chars per line is reached. - Figure it all out, and add QUEUE.md tasks to bring all compiler runs and all examples to use rae format always. Design it well, we don’t want any more examples, which don’t run it. So I think the compiler should by default run it, before it runs all other things. DONE (design + task map; implementation is #916-#919): decisions recorded in docs/rae-format-design.md 'Decisions (#911)' (the 2026-08-30 proposal below it stays as rationale) and a normative 'Source formatting' section in AGENTS.md. The policy: one style, no config — 2-space indent, 100 columns, LF, final newline, 1000-line cap unchanged; parameters/arguments go vertical (one per line, no commas, `)` at declaration indent) from FOUR items or whenever the line would exceed 100 columns, 1-3 may share the line if the whole header/call fits; type fields / object / collection literals the same from FIVE; the COMPILER FORMATS FIRST by default — rae run/build/watch rewrite the project's changed .rae files canonically in place (atomic, mtime untouched when unchanged) before lexing anything, with --check-format / RAE_FORMAT=check refusing to write and failing instead (the suite + example gate use that); a file whose canonical form exceeds 1000 lines is an error to split, never a rewrite; `rae format <path>` writes in place, --stdout prints, plus --check/--stdin/--json/--rules; traversal skips .git, build output and the per-app state folders literally named `.rae`. Survey of the 928 active .rae files that sized the tasks: 746 would change (395 test cases, 178 lib, 173 examples); 16 would be pushed over the 1000-line cap and must be split first (lib/GpuRender 748->1549, Gpu3d 878->1476, webgpu/WebgpuEnums 798->1346, Gpu2dCanvas 895->1338, Gbuffer 840->1296, ui/legacyRaylib/Render 992->1270, GbufferPasses 683->1266, gpu/GpuLifetime 694->1202, ui/Registry 975->1140, water/WaterFft 713->1116, 106 ScreenRouter 962->1076, 114 App 753->1050, 106 Main 994->1044, ui/Frames 903->1034, ui/ThemeResolved 898->1005, 106 DebugGpu2dLocal 806->1003); 303 files have a >100-column line today, 158 still would after formatting; the formatter CRASHES on any char literal (SIGSEGV pp_expr_prec->pp_write_str; lib/Char, Json, String + a one-liner `let c: Char = 'a'`) and is NOT idempotent on a comment after a multi-line call (101_gpu2d_vector, 3 places). pretty.c is 1349 lines. No code changed; verification = the survey script (/tmp, not committed) + reading the existing 200-208/568/786 format fixtures.

- [x] #914 {difficulty:2} [compiler/sema] Diagnose UNDECLARED VALUE identifiers. Today `let n: Int = missing + 1` and `bump(box: box)` (no `box` in scope) pass sema and emit C; only gcc fails with 'use of undeclared identifier'. #821 added the lookup-miss diagnostic for bare FUNCTION names only. Add the same for value identifiers in expression position (locals, params, consts, module-level lets; keep the `__`-prefixed backend-intrinsic exemption), with a fixture (expected diagnostic with file:line:col + a cross-file/open case), and re-run watch-tests.sh; a lib/examples sweep with the new diagnostic on may surface latent cases — fix them in the same commit. Found by #907 (a `paintSubtreeG` call passed an undeclared `box`, caught only at link). DONE: added a bool `is_value_pos` parameter to sema.c's sema_analyze_expr (all 31 call sites updated); its AST_EXPR_IDENT case now diagnoses "unknown value 'name': no local, parameter, const, or global by that name is in scope here" (file:line:col) whenever an identifier used as a genuine VALUE resolves to no Symbol. `is_value_pos` is false ONLY for the 3 positions where a bare identifier legitimately names a module/type/function instead of a value — the call callee, the `.member` qualifier object, and the `.method(...)` receiver object (each already has its own, more precise diagnostic elsewhere, e.g. #821's 'unknown function' / 'not open here' / #788's shadow message) — every other position (binary/unary/cast operands, call ARGUMENT values, let/const/global initializers, return values, assignment target+value, if/loop conditions, for-in collections, match subject+patterns, object-literal field values, list/interp elements, index target+index, box/unbox/own operands) is true. Also exempts `__`-prefixed backend intrinsics (mirrors #821) and bare PRIMITIVE type names (`List.create(Int, cap: n)`) via the existing sema_arg_is_type_name helper — user-declared types/enums/functions/globals need no separate exemption because the module-level pre-population pass already registers them as real Symbols with a decl. New fixture compiler/tests/cases/809_undeclared_value_ident (a cross-file case: Main.rae calls a genuine, auto-open sibling-file function Helper.recordScore but passes an undeclared `bonus` as a named argument value, verifying the diagnostic reports Main.rae's file:line:col, not Helper's). Verified: watch-tests.sh 470/470 (469 existing + the new fixture, all green — the design surfaced zero false positives against the existing 803-fixture repo-internal corpus); confirmed both motivating repros (`let n: Int = missing + 1` and `bump(box: box)`) now fail sema with the precise diagnostic instead of reaching gcc; ran the lib/examples sweep the task asked for (emit-c every one of the 76 non-legacy examples) — all 76 compiled clean with the new diagnostic on, so no latent undeclared-value bugs remain in the actively-maintained tree (the #907 bug this task was filed from was already fixed when its own commit landed).
